href=\"#what-are-memory-blocks\"><span aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"sl-anchor-icon\"><svg width=\"16\" height=\"16\" viewBox=\"0 0 24 24\"><path fill=\"currentcolor\" d=\"m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 They are always visible - no retrieval needed.</p>\n<p>Under the hood, memory blocks are simply prepended to the agent’s prompt in an XML-like format. She is a software engineer who prefers concise answers.&lt;/</span><span style=\"--0:#1E7734;--1:#85E89D\">value</span><span style=\"--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8\">&gt;</span></div></div><div class=\"ec-line\"><div class=\"code\"><span style=\"--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8\">&lt;/</span><span style=\"--0:#1E7734;--1:#85E89D\">human</span><span style=\"--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8\">&gt;</span></div></div><div class=\"ec-line\"><div class=\"code\">\n</div></div><div class=\"ec-line\"><div class=\"code\"><span style=\"--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8\">&lt;/</span><span style=\"--0:#1E7734;--1:#85E89D\">memory_blocks</span><span style=\"--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8\">&gt;</span></div></div></code></pre><div class=\"copy\"><div aria-live=\"polite\"></div><button title=\"Copy to clipboard\" data-copied=\"Copied!\" data-code=\"&lt;memory_blocks&gt;&lt;persona&gt;&lt;description&gt;The persona block: Stores details about your current persona, guiding how you behave and respond.&lt;/description&gt;&lt;metadata&gt;- chars_current=128- chars_limit=5000&lt;/metadata&gt;&lt;value&gt;I am a helpful assistant named Sam. I enjoy helping users solve problems.&lt;/value&gt;&lt;/persona&gt;&lt;human&gt;&lt;description&gt;The human block: Stores key details about the person you are conversing with, allowing for more personalized and friend-like conversation.&lt;/description&gt;&lt;metadata&gt;- chars_current=84- chars_limit=5000&lt;/metadata&gt;&lt;value&gt;The user's name is Alice. She is a software engineer who prefers concise answers.&lt;/value&gt;&lt;/human&gt;&lt;/memory_blocks&gt;\"><div></div></button></div></figure></div>\n<p>This structure is automatically managed by Letta - you define the blocks, and agents can read and update them using <a href=\"/guides/core-concepts/tools/builtin-tools\">built-in memory tools</a>.</p>\n<p><strong>Memory blocks are Letta’s core abstraction.</strong> Create a block with a descriptive label and the agent learns how to use it. This simple mechanism enables capabilities impossible with traditional context management.</p>\n<p><strong>Key properties:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Agent-managed</strong> - Agents autonomously organize information based on block labels</li>\n<li><strong>Flexible</strong> - Use for any purpose: knowledge, guidelines, state tracking, scratchpad space</li>\n<li><strong>Shareable</strong> - Multiple agents can access the same block; update once, visible everywhere (see <a href=\"/tutorials/shared-memory-blocks\">shared memory blocks</a>)</li>\n<li><strong>Always visible</strong> - Blocks stay in context, never need retrieval</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>Examples:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Store tool usage guidelines so agents avoid past mistakes</li>\n<li>Maintain working memory in a scratchpad block</li>\n<li>Mirror external state (user’s current document) for real-time awareness</li>\n<li>Share read-only policies across all agents from a central source</li>\n<li>Coordinate multi-agent systems: parent agents watch subagent result blocks update in real-time</li>\n<li>Enable emergent behavior: add <code dir=\"auto\">performance_tracking</code> or <code dir=\"auto\">emotional_state</code> and watch agents start using them</li>\n</ul>\n<p>Memory blocks aren’t just storage - they’re a coordination primitive that enables sophisticated agent behavior.</p>\n<div class=\"sl-heading-wrapper level-h2\"><h2 id=\"memory-block-structure\">Memory block structure</h2><a class=\"sl-anchor-link\" href=\"#memory-block-structure\"><span aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"sl-anchor-icon\"><svg width=\"16\" height=\"16\" viewBox=\"0 0 24 24\"><path fill=\"currentcolor\" d=\"m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\"></path></svg></span><span class=\"sr-only\" data-pagefind-ignore=\"true\">Section titled “Memory block structure”</span></a></div>\n<p>Memory blocks represent a section of an agent’s context window. An agent may have multiple memory blocks, or none at all. A memory block consists of:</p>\n<ul>\n<li>A <code dir=\"auto\">label</code>, which is a unique identifier for the block</li>\n<li>A <code dir=\"auto\">description</code>, which describes the purpose of the block</li>\n<li>A <code dir=\"auto\">value</code>, which is the contents/data of the block</li>\n<li>A <code dir=\"auto\">limit</code>, which is the size limit (in characters) of the block</li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"sl-heading-wrapper level-h2\"><h2 id=\"the-importance-of-the-description-field\">The importance of the <code dir=\"auto\">description</code> field</h2><a class=\"sl-anchor-link\" href=\"#the-importance-of-the-description-field\"><span aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"sl-anchor-icon\"><svg width=\"16\" height=\"16\" viewBox=\"0 0 24 24\"><path fill=\"currentcolor\" d=\"m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\"></path></svg></span><span class=\"sr-only\" data-pagefind-ignore=\"true\">Section titled “The importance of the description field”</span></a></div>\n<p>When making memory blocks, it’s crucial to provide a good <code dir=\"auto\">description</code> field that accurately describes what the block should be used for.\nThe <code dir=\"auto\">description</code> is the main information used by the agent to determine how to read and write to that block. Without a good description, the agent may not understand how to use the block.</p>\n<p>Because <code dir=\"auto\">persona</code> and <code dir=\"auto\">human</code> are two popular block labels, Letta autogenerates default descriptions for these blocks if you don’t provide them. If you provide a description for a memory block labelled <code dir=\"auto\">persona</code> or <code dir=\"auto\">human</code>, the default description will be overridden.</p>\n<p>For <code dir=\"auto\">persona</code>, a good default is:</p>\n<blockquote>\n<p>The persona block: Stores details about your current persona, guiding how you behave and respond. This helps you to maintain consistency and personality in your interactions.</p>\n</blockquote>\n<p>For <code dir=\"auto\">human</code>, a good default is:</p>\n<blockquote>\n<p>The human block: Stores key details about the person you are conversing with, allowing for more personalized and friend-like conversation.</p>\n</blockquote>\n<div class=\"sl-heading-wrapper level-h2\"><h2 id=\"read-only-blocks\">Read-only blocks</h2><a class=\"sl-anchor-link\" href=\"#read-only-blocks\"><span aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"sl-anchor-icon\"><svg width=\"16\" height=\"16\" viewBox=\"0 0 24 24\"><path fill=\"currentcolor\" d=\"m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\"></path></svg></span><span class=\"sr-only\" data-pagefind-ignore=\"true\">Section titled “Read-only blocks”</span></a></div>\n<p>Memory blocks are read-write by default (so the agent can update the block using memory tools), but can be set to read-only by setting the <code dir=\"auto\">read_only</code> field to <code dir=\"auto\">true</code>. When a block is read-only, the agent cannot update the block.</p>\n<p>Read-only blocks are useful when you want to give an agent access to information (for example, a shared memory block about an organization), but you don’t want the agent to be able to make potentially destructive changes to the block.</p>\n<starlight-tabs data-sync-key=\"language\" class=\"astro-pi6twogl\"> <div For most chat applications, we recommend creating a <code dir=\"auto\">human</code> block (to represent memories about the user) and a <code dir=\"auto\">persona</code> block (to represent the agent’s persona).</p>\n<starlight-tabs data-sync-key=\"language\" class=\"astro-pi6twogl\"> <div class=\"tablist-wrapper not-content astro-pi6twogl\"> <ul role=\"tablist\" class=\"astro-pi6twogl\"> <li role=\"presentation\" class=\"tab astro-pi6twogl\"> <a role=\"tab\" href=\"#tab-panel-129\" id=\"tab-129\" aria-selected=\"true\" tabindex=\"0\" class=\"astro-pi6twogl\">  TypeScript </a> </li><li role=\"presentation\" class=\"tab astro-pi6twogl\"> <a role=\"tab\" href=\"#tab-panel-130\" id=\"tab-130\" aria-selected=\"false\" tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"astro-pi6twogl\">  Python </a> </li> </ul> </div> <div id=\"tab-panel-129\" aria-labelledby=\"tab-129\" role=\"tabpanel\"> <div class=\"expressive-code\"><figure class=\"frame not-content\"><figcaption class=\"header\"></figcaption><pre data-language=\"typescript\"><code><div class=\"ec-line\"><div class=\"code\"><span style=\"--0:#616972;--1:#99A0A6\">// install letta-client with `npm install @letta-ai/letta-client`</span></div></div><div class=\"ec-line\"><div class=\"code\"><span style=\"--0:#BF3441;--1:#F97583\">import</span><span style=\"--0:#24292E;--1:#E1E4E8\"> Letta </span><span agent is created, the corresponding blocks are also created and attached to the agent, so that the block value will be in the context window.</p>\n<div class=\"sl-heading-wrapper level-h2\"><h2 id=\"creating-and-attaching-memory-blocks\">Creating and attaching memory blocks</h2><a class=\"sl-anchor-link\" href=\"#creating-and-attaching-memory-blocks\"><span aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"sl-anchor-icon\"><svg width=\"16\" height=\"16\" viewBox=\"0 0 24 24\"><path fill=\"currentcolor\" d=\"m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 1.42Z\"></path></svg></span><span class=\"sr-only\" data-pagefind-ignore=\"true\">Section titled “Creating and attaching memory blocks”</span></a></div>\n<p>You can also directly create blocks and attach them to an agent. This can be useful if you want to create blocks that are shared between multiple agents. If multiple agents are attached to a block, they will all have the block data in their context windows (essentially providing shared memory).</p>\n<p>Below is an example of creating a block directly, and attaching the block to two agents by specifying the <code dir=\"auto\">block_ids</code> field.</p>\n<starlight-tabs data-sync-key=\"language\" class=\"astro-pi6twogl\"> <div class=\"tablist-wrapper not-content astro-pi6twogl\"> <ul role=\"tablist\" class=\"astro-pi6twogl\"> <li role=\"presentation\" class=\"tab astro-pi6twogl\"> <a role=\"tab\" href=\"#tab-panel-131\" id=\"tab-132\" aria-selected=\"false\" tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"astro-pi6twogl\">  Python </a> </li> </ul> </div> <div id=\"tab-panel-131\" aria-labelledby=\"tab-131\" role=\"tabpanel\"> <div class=\"expressive-code\"><figure class=\"frame not-content\"><figcaption class=\"header\"></figcaption><pre data-language=\"typescript\"><code><div class=\"ec-line\"><div class=\"code\"><span it is not\nan append operation. If multiple processes (agents or external scripts) modify\nthe same block concurrently, the last write wins and overwrites all earlier\nchanges. To avoid data loss:</p><ul>\n<li>Set blocks to <strong>read-only</strong> if you don’t want agents to modify them</li>\n<li>Only modify blocks directly in controlled scenarios where overwriting is acceptable</li>\n<li>Ensure your application logic accounts for full replacements, not merges</li>\n</ul></div></aside>\n<div class=\"sl-heading-wrapper level-h3\"><h3 id=\"deleting-a-block\">Deleting a block</h3><a class=\"sl-anchor-link\" href=\"#deleting-a-block\"><span aria-hidden=\"true\" class=\"sl-anchor-icon\"><svg width=\"16\" height=\"16\" viewBox=\"0 0 24 24\"><path fill=\"currentcolor\" d=\"m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\"></path></svg></span><span class=\"sr-only\" data-pagefind-ignore=\"true\">Section titled “Deleting a block”</span></a></div>\n<p>You can delete a block when it’s no longer needed.
